# Cockpit: the ghostframe chat interface, v7

Design specification for the Claude Code transcript and its docked pane in a terminal. Target: Windows Terminal, Geist Mono Nerd Font 11.5pt, truecolor, background `#0B0B0E`, 60 to 240 columns. Checked against the plugin API types shipped with Claude Code 2.1.329 (`claude-code.d.ts`; v6 was checked against 2.1.328, and every v6 claim reused here was checked again). A claim that comes from those types is marked **(types)**. Everything that still needs a run in a real session is marked **probe n** and collected in section I.

How to read the mocks: every `text` block is exactly the stated width in cells. Hero mocks carry a `paint` block (one token letter per cell, capital = bold, space = blank cell) and a `bg` block (`.` base, `s` surface, `z` zebra, `k` chip). All mocks were generated by a script that fails on any line that is not exactly the stated width, and on any glyph that is not in table D.3. The pane inside every screen mock is not drawn by hand: it is the output of the layout rule in E.37, run on that scenario's data.

Three words are used throughout:

- **docked**: fullscreen mode, the pane sits beside the transcript.
- **inline**: the pane sits above the prompt, because you asked for it where no dock exists.
- **fallback**: no pane. The transcript carries the work as capsules.

---

## A. Thesis

Cockpit splits the screen the way an IDE does, by kind of attention. The left half is a conversation and nothing else: your prompts, Claude's answers, the few things you must act on, the few things that left your machine, and one receipt per turn. The right half is an instrument: what Claude is doing this second, every step of the turn with its result, the agents, the plan, the files and the sources. The left is append-only and you read it. The right changes freely and you glance at it. A 300-step turn costs the left side one receipt row and costs the right side nothing it has not already reserved.

The two halves are one instrument because they share one grid. Each has a spine in its column 0, a blank gutter, content from column 2, and a time edge on its last column. Each uses the same glyphs, the same kind words, the same state words and the same clock format. A row that moves from the pane to the transcript (when the window gets narrow, or when you print a step log) does not change shape.

The pane is a convenience and never a dependency. Every fact in it can also be reached as text (ctrl+o, `/steps 7 all`), nothing that needs you is ever shown only there, and when no dock exists the same facts fold into one **capsule** row per stretch of work, plus a row for each exception. That fallback is designed as carefully as the docked view, because it is what you get in tmux, below 110 columns, and outside fullscreen.

---

## B. Principles

| # | Rule | Test a developer can run |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **One spine, twice.** In each half, column 0 holds at most one glyph, column 1 is blank, text starts in column 2. | Regex every drawn row of the transcript, and every row of the pane body, against `^(.\| ) ` (or an indent of 2n spaces); zero failures. |
| 2 | **The left is a conversation.** A transcript row is one of five things: your words, Claude's words, something you must act on, something that left the machine, a receipt. A step is none of these. | Run 50 passing `Read` calls docked: the transcript grows by 0 rows; the pane's `[ STEPS ]` count grows by 50. |
| 3 | **The right is an instrument.** It shows state. Nothing exists only there. | Close the pane mid-turn (ctrl+x x). Every count and every failure is still reachable: receipt, capsule, ctrl+o, `/steps n all`. |
| 4 | **Append-only left, calm right.** Rows above the newest transcript block never change. The pane changes, but its sections never move during a turn, its rows never reorder, and it never animates. | Record a turn at 30 fps. Left: only appended rows differ. Right: no section label changes its row between `turn.start` and `turn.complete`, unless a section appears for the first time. |
| 5 | **Time lives on the right edge of each half.** Durations and clocks are right-aligned to the last column of their half and nowhere else in a row. | The last non-blank cell of every receipt, turn rule, capsule and step row is in the last column of its half. |
| 6 | **Words carry meaning, colour confirms it.** Every state is a word, every row kind a distinct glyph. | Render with `NO_COLOR=1`; every state in section E is still distinguishable in a screenshot of either half. |
| 7 | **One glyph, one meaning, both halves.** No glyph in table D has a second purpose, and the pane uses no glyph the transcript does not. | Grep the source for each glyph; every use maps to its row in table D. |
| 8 | **No walls.** Frames and sections have a top rule and nothing at their sides. The only vertical line on screen is the engine's divider between the halves. | Mouse-select a code block and paste: the result differs from the source only by a uniform 2-space indent. |
| 9 | **Colour is rationed.** Accent is only for you. Green, pink and amber only for a result. Lavender only for agents, teal only for web hosts, blue only for URLs. | Screenshot a 20-step passing turn, docked: the only non-grey cells are the accent bar, the cursor and the spinner. |
| 10 | **Say it once.** A fact has one home on screen. The step in progress is in the pane, the turn clock is on the left, the model is in the status line. | Search a docked screenshot for any number or name; it appears once outside the prose. |
| 11 | **Same turn, every mode.** Docked, inline and fallback show the same facts. Only the place changes. | Resize 200 → 100 mid-turn: every count the pane showed is now in a capsule, the live area or a receipt. Resize back: the reverse. |
| 12 | **Nothing that needs you is only in the pane.** Permissions, questions, plans, limits and failures that were never fixed reach you on the left or at the prompt. | Close the pane and run section F's scenarios 9, 12, 14, 19 and 37: you are stopped, and told why, in each. |

---

## C. Grid

### C.1 Columns

Let `C` be the terminal width in cells. When a pane is docked it takes `P` body cells plus a 2-cell chrome that the engine draws (a divider and a pad: **probe 17**). The transcript's region is `L = C − P − 2` cells, or `C` when nothing is docked. The frame is `F = min(L − 1, 120)`; the last column of the region stays empty. All positions are 0-based, and the pane's positions are relative to its own body.

| Name | Transcript | Pane | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| spine | column 0 | column 0 | one glyph or blank |
| gutter | column 1 | column 1 | always blank |
| content | columns 2 … F−1 | columns 2 … P−1 | |
| prose measure | columns 2 … min(F−1, 89) | — | at most 88 cells; the pane holds no prose |
| time edge | ends at F−1, 7 cells + 1 gap | ends at P−1, 7 cells + 2 gap | `59m 59s` is the longest clock |
| result | — | ends 2 cells before the time edge | right-aligned, at most 16 cells |
| rule | columns 0 … F−1 | columns 0 … P−1 | turn rules, frame rules, section rules start on the spine |
| inset | +2 per level | +2 per level | nested list, nested agent, detail row |
| live area | columns 0 … F−1 docked, 0 … C−6 otherwise | — | undocked, the band loses 5 cells to the engine's `[-]` mark **(types)** |

How wide the pane is. Cockpit asks the engine for the widest of 72, 56 or 40 body columns that still leaves the transcript 100 cells, with `$.ui.open({ columns })`. The engine grants at most 45% of the terminal and at least 24, and a width you dragged the dock to wins over the request **(types)**. So the pane must draw at any width from 24 to 90; 40, 56 and 72 are the three it is designed at, and the row formats in E.39 to E.43 say what gives way between them.

| C | mode | P | L | F | prose | notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 60 | fallback | — | 60 | 59 | 57 | capsules name two kinds instead of three |
| 80 | fallback | — | 80 | 79 | 77 | |
| 100 | fallback | — | 100 | 99 | 88 | |
| 110 | docked only if you asked once | 40 | 68 | 67 | 65 | the engine docks from 110 only for a pane you opened yourself **(types)** |
| 144 | docked | 40 | 102 | 101 | 88 | the engine docks an unasked pane from 144 **(types)** |
| 160 | docked | 56 | 102 | 101 | 88 | |
| 200 | docked | 72 | 126 | 120 | 88 | the hero width |
| 240 | docked | 72 | 166 | 120 | 88 | the transcript does not widen past 120; the pane does not widen past 72 unless you drag it |

Rules that follow from the table:

- The transcript never centres and never gains a left margin. Engine rows start at column 0, so a margin would create a second grid.
- From `F ≥ 99`, prose stops at column 89 and the last cells of the frame hold only time and meta: the meta margin.
- Outside fullscreen there is no dock at any width: the engine seats a pane inline above the prompt **(types)**. Cockpit does not open one there unasked; it uses the fallback, and `/steps` opens the inline inspector (E.46).
- Between 110 and 143 columns the pane docks only after you have opened it once with `/cockpit`; the engine remembers that until you close the pane by hand **(types)**.

### C.2 Vertical rhythm

Blank rows between a block (row heading) and the block that follows it (column heading) in the transcript. `·` means the pair cannot occur.

| above ↓ / below → | turn rule | prompt | capsule or event row | prose | frame | receipt | engine row |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **turn rule** | · | 1 | · | · | · | · | 1 |
| **prompt** | · | 0 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 |
| **capsule or event row** | · | 1 | 0 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 0 or 1, engine-owned |
| **prose** | · | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 |
| **frame** | · | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 |
| **receipt** | 2 | · | · | · | · | · | 1 |
| **engine row** | 2 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 0 |

Inside blocks: a frame's label rule, content and closing rule touch. A heading has 1 blank above (2 for a first-level heading) and 0 below. List items touch. The live area is separated from the transcript by exactly 1 blank row, and from the prompt by 0. The only 2 in the matrix is the gap before a turn rule: the widest space on screen always means "a new turn".

In the pane: a section's label rule and its rows touch. Sections are separated by 1 blank row when the pane has 24 body rows or more, and by none below that. The pane's first body row and the transcript's first row are the same terminal row, so the two halves share every baseline; there is no half-row offset anywhere.

---

## D. Tokens

### D.1 Colour

Unchanged from v6, including both contrast fixes. No token was added for the pane.

| Token | Paint | Value | Contrast on bg | Used for |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| bg | `.` | `#0B0B0E` | — | base, in both halves |
| surface | `s` | `#13131A` | — | prompt rows, code rows, diff rows in the pane |
| chip | `k` | `#1E1E26` | — | inline code, the selected option, the focused row of the pane |
| zebra | `z` | `#0F0F13` | — | every second table row |
| hairline | `h` | `#2C2C36` | 1.4:1 | rules, the divider |
| rule-strong | `H` | `#3A3A48` | 1.8:1 | the capsule bar, the table header rule, the divider while the pane has the keyboard |
| text | `t` | `#B2B2BC` | 9.3:1 | answer prose, event rows, step subjects |
| text-bright | `b` | `#ECECF1` | 16.7:1 | your prompt, lead sentence, headings, the step in progress |
| muted | `m` | `#7C7C88` (v5: `#686874`) | 4.8:1 | capsule text, kind words, labels, meta |
| dim | `d` | `#5E5E6A` (v5: `#4E4E5A`) | 3.1:1 | clocks, frame brackets, hints, the `… n earlier` row |
| accent | `a` | `#FAB387` | 11.1:1 | you: the prompt bar, your decisions, the cursor, the spinner |
| link | `l` | `#8FB8DE` | 9.4:1 | URLs and footnote marks |
| ok | `g` | `#96D292` | 11.2:1 | a check that passed, added lines |
| warn | `w` | `#E8B98A` | 11.0:1 | needs you, blocked, limits |
| err | `r` | `#EC84A0` | 7.8:1 | failed, removed lines |
| agent | `v` | `#B4A0DE` | 8.4:1 | agent names |
| web | `c` | `#7EC8BE` | 10.2:1 | web hosts |

`muted` now carries more than it did in v6 (every kind word and most of a 40-column pane), and it was already raised to AA for body text for that reason. `dim` carries every clock on both time edges, at the 3:1 floor. `warn` and `accent` stay close in hue and apart in role: accent is a bar, a cursor or the spinner; warn is always a word. Probe 14 still decides whether `warn` moves to `#E5C07B`.

### D.2 Attributes

| Attribute | Paint | Meaning | Never used for |
|---|---|---|---|
| bold | capital letter | headings; the one word in a row that names its state (`failed`, `blocked`, `needs you`) | emphasis inside capsules or step rows |
| dim (SGR 2) | — | not used; the dim *token* is a colour, so it survives terminals that ignore SGR 2 | |
| italic | — | a title of a work in prose, and `thought` text in the unfolded view | state |
| underline | — | the URL in a Sources row | headings |
| inverse | — | the text cursor; the selected row of a menu and the focused row of the pane under `NO_COLOR`. The engine also inverts the element that holds the pane's focus ring **(types)** | decoration |

### D.3 Glyphs

Spine glyphs (column 0 of either half):

| Glyph | Code point | One meaning | Left | Right | Drawn by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `▍` | U+258D | you: a prompt, a steer, a decision you made | prompt and decision rows | a step you decided (`denied`, an answer, a plan verdict, `cut`) | plugin |
| `│` | U+2502 | the rail: work, folded | the capsule (fallback); a sentence Claude said while working (unfolded view) | a sentence Claude said while working (full view) | plugin |
| `✕` | U+2715 | failed | a failure that was never fixed; any failure, in fallback | the step that failed; an agent that failed | plugin |
| `⊘` | U+2298 | blocked by a system (classifier, hook, sandbox, policy) | a block that was never resolved; any block, in fallback | the step that was blocked | plugin |
| `!` | U+0021 | needs you: the turn cannot continue without you | limits, context | the step that is waiting on you; the `[ NOW ]` row | plugin |
| `✓` | U+2713 | a check passed (tests, types, CI) | fallback only | the check that passed after a failure, or the last check before the answer | plugin |
| `↗` | U+2197 | published outward (push, pull request, merge, release) | always, at once | the step that did it | plugin |
| `±` | U+00B1 | a recorded change (commit) | — | the step that committed | plugin |
| `↺` | U+21BA | rewound to a checkpoint | the rewind row | — | plugin |
| `◇` | U+25C7 | an agent | engine rows; the fallback roster | the roster | engine and plugin |
| `●` | U+25CF | a notice from outside the turn | notices | — | engine and plugin |
| `›` | U+203A | a message from an agent (engine) | engine row | — | engine |
| `※` | U+203B | a recap (engine) | engine row | — | engine |
| `∴` | U+2234 | thought | unfolded view | a `thought` row in `[ STEPS ]` | plugin |
| `▸` | U+25B8 | the current item of a list | the selected option, the current to-do (fallback band) | the step in progress, the current to-do, the `[ NOW ]` sentence | plugin |
| `⠋⠙⠹⠸⠼⠴⠦⠧⠇⠏` | U+280B… | running, now | the live now-row only | never | plugin |

`▸` moved from the inline table to the spine table: in the pane it sits in column 0, and "the step in progress" is the current item of the step list, which is the meaning it always had. The spinner stays out of the pane on purpose (principle 4).

Inline glyphs:

| Glyph | One meaning |
|---|---|
| `─` | a turn boundary (solid rule): the opening rule and the receipt |
| `┄` | a frame or section boundary (dotted rule) |
| `[ ]` around capitals | the label of a rule |
| `·` | separator between fields of one row; an uncited source |
| `…` | text was cut here; `… n earlier`, `… n more` |
| `↩` | this row continues an unbreakable token from the row above |
| `→` | becomes (before → after, question → answer, failure → fixed) |
| `▰` `▱` | one filled / one empty cell of a meter (status line only) |
| `▏` | a quotation |
| `¹ ² ³` | a footnote mark, in the answer and beside the same source in the pane |
| `[ ]` `[x]` `[>]` | to-do: open, done, in progress |
| `+` `-` at column 2 of a diff | added line, removed line |
| `−` (U+2212) | a negative number, removed-line counts |
| `█` | the text cursor |
| `[×]` | stand-in for the engine's close mark on the dock (**probe 17** for its real look) |

Nerd Font private-use icons are still not used anywhere in the transcript or the pane: they do not survive copy and paste, and each needs a word beside it anyway. **The status line keeps its Nerd Font icons** (E.35): nothing there is ever copied, and the script already owns that look.

Kind words (the fixed vocabulary for a step; always lower case, always muted, at most 8 cells): `read` `edit` `write` `search` `shell` `test` `web` `fetch` `mcp` `skill` `hook` `notebook` `browser` `agent` `ask` `plan` `todo`. Three more appear only in `[ NOW ]`, where no tool is running: `thinking` `writing` `waiting`.

State words (the fixed vocabulary for a result): `done` `failed` `blocked` `denied` `needs you` `interrupted` `waiting` `running` `queued` `skipped`. Two compounds are built from them and used only on the left: `still failing`, `still blocked` (E.5).

---

## E. Components

Each component lists: purpose, anatomy (an 80-column mock for the left half, a 72-column mock for the pane, unless stated), states, streaming or update behaviour, narrow behaviour, and the render site. Each heading says what happened to the component since v6: **kept** (the v6 rules hold as written; they are restated so this document stands alone), **changed**, or **new**. "Site" names follow the plugin API; **probe n** points at section I.

Where something lives, decided case by case:

| Thing | Docked | Fallback | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| Your prompt, steer, queued message | left | left | they are the conversation |
| Claude's answer, an interim report | left | left | they are the conversation |
| A sentence Claude says while working | pane `[ NOW ]`, then the full view | live now-row, then ctrl+o | it describes the work, it is not addressed to you |
| A passing step | pane | counted in the capsule | the expected case |
| A failed or blocked step that is later fixed | pane (`✕`, then `✓`); the receipt says `1 failure → fixed` | `✕` row at once; the capsule says `→ fixed` | it did not change the answer; without a pane the row is the only live signal |
| A failure or block that is never fixed | pane, and a `still failing` row above the receipt | the `✕` row it already drew | it changed the answer (principle 12) |
| Permission prompt | the engine's dialog at the prompt; pane `[ NOW ]` shows the step that asked | dialog and a live `! needs you` row | you must act |
| Your permission decision | left for `denied` and `always allow`; pane only for `allowed once` | the same | a denial changes the answer, a standing rule changes the future, a single allow changes nothing |
| Question card and its answer | card at the prompt, answer row left | the same | you must act; your answer is your words |
| Plan and your verdict | left | left | a plan is a document you approve |
| Commit | pane (`±`), and the answer usually names it | counted in the capsule | local and reversible |
| Push, pull request, merge, release, deploy | left, at once, and the pane step (`↗`) | left, at once | it left the machine; you will scroll back for the link |
| Agents | pane roster | live roster, then the capsule | work |
| To-do list | pane | one live row; `/todo` prints it | work |
| Files changed | pane; count in the receipt | count in the receipt; `/files` prints the table | work |
| Sources that the answer cites | left, in the answer's `[ SOURCES ]` frame, and marked in the pane | left | answer content |
| Pages read but not cited | pane only | ctrl+o | work |
| API errors, limits, context warnings | left | left | you must act or wait |
| Notices (MCP, updates, memory saves, compaction, resume) | left | left | they are about the session, not a turn |
| Thinking | pane | live now-row | time, not content |

### E.1 Turn boundary · changed

**Purpose.** Make a turn one unit: a solid labelled rule opens it, a solid receipt closes it. Solid rules appear nowhere else.

```text
─ [ TURN 7 ] ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── 10:26 
                                                                                
▍ the width tests fail on CI but pass locally, find out why and fix it          
                                                                                
  CI was running Bun 1.4.1, whose bundled ICU splits a skin-tone modifier into  
  its own grapheme. The tests and the code were right; the runner was old.      
                                                                                
─ done · turn 7 ─────────────── 20 steps · 1 failure → fixed · 3 files · 2m 44s 
```
```paint
h d bbbb b d hhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhhh mmmmm 
                                                                                
a bbb bbbbb bbbbb bbbb bb bb bbb bbbb bbbbbbbb bbbb bbb bbb bbb bbb bb          
                                                                                
  bb bbb bbbbbbb bbb bbbbbb bbbbb bbbbbbb bbb bbbbbb b bbbbbbbbb bbbbbbbb bbbb  
  bbb bbb bbbbbbbbb ttt ttttt ttt ttt tttt tttt tttttt ttt tttttt ttt tttt      
                                                                                
h mmmm m mmmm m hhhhhhhhhhhhhhh mm mmmmm m m mmmmmmm m mmmmm m m mmmmm m tt ttt 
```
```bg
................................................................................
................................................................................
sssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssss.
................................................................................
................................................................................
................................................................................
................................................................................
................................................................................
```

- **Anatomy.** Opening rule: `─` at column 0, `[ TURN n ]` from column 2, rule fill, wall clock (24h) ending on the time edge. Closing rule (the receipt, E.32): `─`, the state word and `· turn n` from column 2, rule fill, then counts and the total duration ending on the time edge.
- **What changed.** The receipt repeats the turn number, because the number is now an address: `/steps 7` pins that turn in the pane, and on a long answer the opening rule is far above the receipt.
- **States of the label.** `[ TURN 14 ]`; `[ TURN 15 · RESUMED ]`; `[ TURN 31 · AFTER COMPACT ]`; `[ TURN 13 · AFTER REWIND ]`. When the calendar day differs from the previous turn's, the clock becomes `Thu 19 Nov · 09:02`.
- **Streaming.** The opening rule draws once, with the prompt. The receipt draws once, at turn end. If a turn has no receipt (probe 3), the next turn rule closes it.
- **Narrow.** Below 72 columns the receipt drops fields from the left of its meta (E.32). Duration and state word are never dropped.
- **Site.** Opening rule: `UserMessage` where `origin.kind` is `composer`, as the first row of its tree. Receipt: `TurnDuration`.

**Purpose.** Your words are the landmark you scroll for. They get the only accent glyph and the only full-width background on the left.

```bg
sssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssssss.
```

- **Anatomy.** `▍` (accent) in column 0 on every row of the prompt, text from column 2 in text-bright, `surface` background from column 0 to F−1. The background stops at F: it never runs under the divider.
- **States.** *Short*: one row. *Multi-line*: one bar per row, wrapped at the block measure, never truncated up to 6 rows. *Long paste*: the first 3 rows, then one muted row `… n more lines · pasted · ctrl+o`; the cut is decided at first draw. *@file mention* and *attachment*: a chip with no padding cells; an image is `[ image n ]`. *Steer* and *queued*: E.20.
- **Narrow.** Wraps at F. A path chip that does not fit moves whole to the next row; a chip longer than the row is cut in the middle (`hooks/…/register.tsx`).
- **Site.** `UserMessage`. Its `isExpanded` prop is true in the ctrl+o view **(types)**, where the full paste is drawn.

### E.3 The capsule · new (replaces the rail)

**Purpose.** Fallback mode's whole account of a stretch of work, in one row. It is what the pane's `[ STEPS ]` section becomes when there is no pane.

```text
│ worked · 20 steps · 6 shell · 5 read · 2 test +7 · 1 failure → fixed   2m 36s 
                                                                                
│ worked · 2 steps · 2 read                                                  3s 
│ worked · 1 step · 1 edit                                                 0.4s 
│ worked · 312 steps · 141 shell · 96 read +75 · 3 failures → fixed     51m 02s 
│ worked · 9 steps · 4 read · 3 shell · 2 edit · 1 failure                  48s 
│ worked · 7 agents · 214 steps · 6 done · 1 failed                     13m 42s 
│ worked · 14 steps · 9 read · 5 search · 1 blocked                      1m 10s 
│ thought                                                                   48s 
```

```paint
H mmmmmm m mmm mmmmm m mmm mmmmm m mm mmmm mmm m m mmmmmmmm m mmmmm     ddd ddd 
H mmmmmm m m mmmmm m m mmmm m m mmmmm m m mmmm m r rrrrrrr                  ddd 
H mmmmmm m m mmmmmm m mmm mmmmm m m mmmm m r rrrrrr                     ddd ddd 
H mmmmmm m mm mmmmm m m mmmm m m mmmmmm m w wwwwwww                      dd ddd 
H mmmmmmm                                                                   ddd 
```
```bg
................................................................................
................................................................................
................................................................................
................................................................................
................................................................................
................................................................................
................................................................................
................................................................................
................................................................................
```

```text
│ worked · 20 steps · 1 failure → fixed              2m 36s 
│ worked · 312 steps · 3 failures → fixed           51m 02s 
│ worked · 20 steps · 1 failure → fixed                     
│ worked · 9 steps · 4 read +5 · 1 failure                  
```

- **Anatomy.** `│` (rule-strong) in column 0. Then, in muted: `worked`, the step count, up to three kinds by count (`6 shell · 5 read · 2 test`) and `+n` for the rest, and the exception story. The turn clock at which the stretch ended is on the time edge, dim.
- **The exception story** is the last field and the only coloured one: `1 failure → fixed` (muted: it ended well), `1 failure` (err), `1 blocked` (warn), `7 agents · 6 done · 1 failed`. Zero exceptions: the field is absent.
- **When a capsule is drawn.** It closes a stretch of work, and it is always drawn by something that is being drawn anyway, so nothing is edited afterwards:
  1. when a block of Claude's text that stays in the transcript starts (the answer, or an interim report): the capsule is the first row of that block's tree, and at that moment every step before it has finished;
  2. when 5 minutes have passed with no new transcript row and a step ends: that step's own site draws a capsule for the stretch so far, so a long silent turn leaves a trail (scenario F.5 has nine of them in 51 minutes);
  3. when a turn ends without any text: the receipt's site draws it above the receipt.
- **What it never does.** It is never updated. A stretch that is still running is in the live area (E.33), not in a capsule.
- **Zero steps:** no capsule. **Thinking only:** `│ thought` and the time, when thinking took more than 10 seconds.
- **Narrow.** Kinds give way first, from the right, folding into `+n`: three kinds from 72 columns, two below, then one, then none. Below 60 columns the clock is dropped too (the receipt keeps the total). The step count and the exception story are never cut (the last two rows of the second mock are 50 columns wide).
- **Not drawn when docked.** The pane has the steps and the receipt has the counts (principle 10).
- **Site.** `AssistantMessage` (case 1), `ToolUse` (case 2), `TurnDuration` (case 3). Case 1 depends on the same fact as v6's fold: knowing that a text block will stay (probe 1).

### E.4 The unfolded view (ctrl+o) · changed

**Purpose.** The complete record as plain text: every sentence, every step under the sentence that announced it, every turn, in order.

```text
│ Lint is clean. Running zizmor with the auditor persona (as CI does)    1m 12s 
│ and the format check, then opening the PR.                                    
│   shell    zizmor --persona auditor .github/workflows          clean     4.1s 
│   shell    oxfmt --check .                            3 files differ     0.9s 
│   shell    git commit -m "ci: precise workflow names"        a1f3c2e     0.3s 
│   shell    gh pr create --fill                                 PR #5     2.2s 
│   ∴ thought The format check failed and I committed anyway.                6s 
```

```bg
................................................................................
................................................................................
```

- **How ctrl+o works in Cockpit.** It is still the engine's switch. In that view the engine raises one `ToolUse` row per call and folds nothing **(types)**, and the plugin draws each row as below instead of passing it to the engine's grey slab. The pane stays where it is and keeps showing one turn; ctrl+o shows all of them inline. The two differ by job: the pane is live and you glance at it; the unfolded view is still, and you read, search and copy it.
- **Anatomy.** A sentence Claude said while working is a `│` row in muted with the turn clock; it wraps instead of being cut. Step rows continue the bar and are inset to column 4: kind word (muted, 8 cells), subject (text), result word right-aligned at column F−10, the step's *own* duration on the time edge (the only place it is shown; everywhere else the clock is the turn's).
- **Result words.** A result is a noun phrase taken from the tool's output, never `ok`: line counts for `read`, `+n −n` for `edit`, the exit summary for `shell`, `n pass` or `n of m fail` for `test`, the HTTP status for `fetch`, `n matches` for `search`. Colour only when it is not the expected result.
- **Diffs and output** appear here in full, in frames (E.8), directly under the step that produced them (scenario F.8).
- **Same rows as the pane's full view** (E.44). `/steps 7 all` prints exactly these rows for one turn into the transcript, as a command's output.
- **Narrow.** Result word drops below 72 columns; duration below 60.
- **Site.** `ToolUse` with `isExpanded` true **(types)**; `AssistantMessage` for the sentences.

Docked (above). In fallback every failure and block draws at once, because there is no pane to show it:

```text
✕ failed · test · bun test width · 2 of 14                                  41s 
    expect(width("👍🏽")).toBe(2) · received 4                                    
    tests/width.test.tsx:41:27                                                  
⊘ blocked · write tests/width.test.tsx · auto-mode classifier            1m 30s 
↗ pushed fix/ci-bun-pin · 1 commit · origin                              2m 40s 
│ worked · 23 steps · 7 shell +16 · 1 failure → fixed · 1 blocked        2m 44s 
```

- **Anatomy.** Glyph (coloured) in column 0; an optional bold state word followed by ` · `; the kind word and subject in text; the turn clock on the time edge. Up to 3 detail rows may follow at column 4 in muted: the decisive lines of the error, chosen at draw time.
- **Which results get a row, and when.**

| Row | Docked | Fallback |
|---|---|---|
| `↗` push, pull request (with its URL on a detail row), merge, release, deploy | at once | at once |
| `▍ denied`, `▍ always allow` | at once | at once |
| `!` limit, context, final API error | at once | at once |
| `✕ failed` | not drawn; the pane has it | at once |
| `⊘ blocked` | not drawn; the pane has it | at once |
| `✕ still failing`, `⊘ still blocked` | at turn end, above the receipt, for each failure or block that nothing later fixed | not needed: the row above already exists |
| `✓` a passing check | never; the receipt says `→ fixed` | never; the capsule says `→ fixed` |
| `±` commit | never; it is a pane mark and usually a sentence of the answer | never |

- **Why `still failing` is honest.** At the moment a test fails nobody knows whether it will be fixed. Docked, the pane shows it that second. The transcript waits until the turn ends, and then states only what stayed true. A row drawn at turn end carries the clock of the failure, not of the turn end, so it still reads as a timeline.
- **"Fixed" means:** a later step of the same kind and subject (the same command, the same file) succeeded in this turn. Anything else is still open.
- **No row is ever amended.** Recovery is a count in the receipt or capsule, never a word written back onto a row.
- **Narrow.** Subject is cut with `…` before the clock; the state word and glyph are never cut.
- **Site.** `ToolUse` for rows drawn at once; `TurnDuration` for the rows above the receipt (probe 3: whether its tree may be several rows).

### E.6 Thinking · changed

**Purpose.** Thinking is time, not content. It is shown as time, in the pane.

```text
┄ [ NOW ] ┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄ turn 12 · step 12
▸ Working out why the quantifier backtracks.                            
  thinking                                                           48s
                                                                        
┄ [ STEPS ] ┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄┄ 12 steps
  read     hooks/md/heading.ts                         96 lines   1m 02s
∴ thought                                                         1m 50s
  edit     hooks/md/heading.ts                            +2 −2   1m 58s
```

- **Running.** `[ NOW ]` keeps the last sentence Claude said and shows `thinking` with its own elapsed time on the second row. The left now-row says `working` as always.
- **After.** One `∴ thought` row in `[ STEPS ]` with the turn clock, only when the thinking took more than 10 seconds. Shorter thinking leaves nothing.
- **Unfolded and full view.** `∴ thought` plus the summary text in muted italic (E.4).
- **Fallback.** No row. The live now-row says `thinking`, and a capsule that covers only thinking says `│ thought`.
- **Site.** `Spinner` (`mode` is `thinking` **(types)**) feeds the pane's state; expanded: probe 6.

### E.7 The answer · kept

**Purpose.** The thing you asked for. Blank spine, text weight, full structure. In Cockpit it is nearly the only thing on the left.

```text
  Colocation works, so the tests can move next to the code they cover. The      
  runner discovers *.test.tsx anywhere under hooks/, and validate ignores them. 
                                                                                
  What moves                                                                    
  - 26 test files go from tests/ to the folder of their module                  
  - shared fixtures stay in tests/kit/                                          
    - seedState() and fakeClock() are imported by 19 files                      
  - nothing changes for CI: the glob is already **/*.test.tsx                   
                                                                                
  Order                                                                         
  1. Move the 7 pure-helper tests first (no fixtures).                          
  2. Move the hook tests, one module per commit.                                
                                                                                
  ▏ Tests reach register only through the engine, so the split is safe.         
```

```bg
................................................................................
...................kkkkkkkkkk................kkkkkk......kkkkkkkk...............
................................................................................
................................................................................
..........................kkkkkk................................................

................................................kkkkkkkkkkkkk...................

```

- **Lead sentence.** The first sentence of the last prose block of a turn is text-bright; it should be the verdict. The plugin does not write it, it only lights it.
- **Verdict.** The state word of the receipt directly under the answer. Lead above, verdict below, body between.
- **Headings.** Level 1: bold text-bright with a hairline row under it and 2 blank rows above. Level 2: bold text-bright. Level 3: bold text. Level 4 and deeper: bold muted. No `#` marks are drawn.
- **Lists.** `-` at the content column, text 2 cells in; nesting is +2 per level, to 4 levels, then flattened. Numbers are right-aligned to the widest number known at first draw. Task lists draw `[ ]` and `[x]` as typed.
- **Quotes.** `▏` at column 2 in muted, body at column 4 in muted.
- **Inline code.** Text-bright on `chip`, with no padding cells, so the grid is not shifted and copy is exact. With `NO_COLOR` the backticks are drawn instead.
- **Interim report.** A block of Claude's text that is followed by more work stays in the transcript when it is structured (it has a table, a code fence, a list or a heading) or longer than 3 wrapped rows. It draws like an answer without the bright lead. Short plain narration does not stay: it goes to `[ NOW ]` (E.38).
- **Streaming.** Rows append at the bottom. A word that would overflow moves to a new row before it is drawn, so no row reflows. Every prose block starts bright; if a tool call follows and the block is narration, the block leaves the transcript in one frame (the fold, probe 1) and its sentence appears in the pane.
- **Narrow.** Prose wraps at F. Nesting insets shrink to +1 per level below 60 columns.
- **Site.** `AssistantMessage`.
